lets give the small section at the right of the card for x button and make it centered. so and the small section at the left to show the number, and make it centered. now visualise how it will look.

> AGENT

```
┌───┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┬───┐
│   │                                             │   │
│   │   HOW MANY?           [ − 2 + ]             │   │
│ 1 │                                             │ × │
│   │   EXPIRES   [📅 15 Aug 2026 · in 4 months]  │   │
│   │                                             │   │
└───┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┴───┘
   ↑                                                ↑
   left rail                                  right rail
   (~40 px wide,                              (~40 px wide,
    centered "1")                             centered "×")
```

Three vertical lanes:
- **Left rail (~40 px)** — batch number, vertically centered, neutral background tint. Big enough to read (titleMedium), but slim so the card doesn't waste horizontal space.
- **Middle (flex 1)** — the actual fields. Same `HOW MANY?` / `EXPIRES` labels + controls as Option 1.
- **Right rail (~40 px)** — X delete button, vertically centered, large hit area, ghost icon that tints red on press. Only shown when `batches.length > 1` (so you can't delete the last batch).

Visual touches:
- Both rails share the card's border but have a 1px divider toward the middle column — gives the "lane" feel without extra borders.
- Slightly muted background on the rails (`surfaceContainerLow`) vs the middle (`surfaceContainerLowest`) so the rails feel like "rails", not part of the form.

With two batches stacked it'd look like:

```
┌───┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┬───┐
│   │   HOW MANY?           [ − 2 + ]             │   │
│ 1 │                                             │ × │
│   │   EXPIRES   [📅 15 Aug 2026 · in 4 months]  │   │
└───┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┴───┘
┌───┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┬───┐
│   │   HOW MANY?           [ − 3 + ]             │   │
│ 2 │                                             │ × │
│   │   EXPIRES   [📅 10 Sep 2026 · in 5 months]  │   │
└───┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┴───┘

       [ + Add another expiry batch ]
```

> AGENT

Clean. Final card structure:

```
┌────┬─────────────────────────────────────┬────┐
│    │                                     │    │
│    │  HOW MANY?                          │    │
│ 1  │  [ − 2 + ]                          │ 🗑 │
│    │                                     │    │
│    │  EXPIRES                            │    │
│    │  [📅 15 Aug 2026 · in 4 months · ×] │    │
│    │                                     │    │
└────┴─────────────────────────────────────┴────┘
```

Details applied:
- **Three lanes**: 44 px left rail, flex middle, 44 px right rail. Equal width so the card stays symmetrical even when the trash isn't shown (then the right rail renders muted/empty).
- **Rails** use `surfaceContainerLow` (slightly elevated tint), the middle uses `surfaceContainerLowest` — gives the "lane" feel without extra borders. Internal 1px dividers tinted with `outlineVariant`.
- **Batch number**: bold `titleMedium`, just `1`, `2`, `3` — no `#`, no debug feel.
- **Trash icon**: `Trash2` (more recognizable than a plain X), tinted `palette.error`, with Android ripple feedback. The whole rail is the tap target, so a sloppy thumb still hits.
- **Section labels** (`HOW MANY?` / `EXPIRES`): small uppercase muted text, consistent with field-app conventions elsewhere (e.g. `PICK FROM BATCH`, `TRACKING MODE`).
- **Card has `overflow: 'hidden'`** so the rails paint flush against the rounded border.

Removed the unused `IconButton` import. Type-check clean.

> DEVELOPER

okay, now how do I test the unit dispense and pack dispense system?

> AGENT

Here's a complete test plan. Run it in this order — earlier steps set up state for later ones.

## Prerequisites (one-time)

1. **Deploy/restart the engine** so the schema migration runs and `dispense_mode` columns get added:
   ```
   cd office-ops-engine
   bun run dev   # or your normal restart flow
   ```
   Sanity check: hit `/api/inventory/canonical-products` — every product should come back with `"dispense_mode": "pack"`.

2. **Run field-app on Metro** so v9 migration runs against the local SQLite:
   ```
   cd field-app && npm start
   ```
   Open app, log in. (Migration runs at first `getDb()`.)

3. **Open dashboard** in browser at the deployed URL (or `bun run dev`).

---

## Test 1 — Pack mode (regression: nothing should change)

Pick any existing catalog SKU with `pack_size > 1` (e.g. one with `unit=g, pack_size=500`).

**Receive 4 packs**:
- Field-app → Receiving → set qty to 4, pick an expiry → save.
- ✅ Total on the BatchEditor should read **`Total: 4 × 500 g`** (not "4 g").
- ✅ Stock screen shows `4` with `× 500 g` under it.

**Dispense 2 packs**:
- DispenseScreen → select the SKU.
- ✅ Card shows **`4 × 500 g on hand`**.
- ✅ Big `[− 1 +]` stepper, no decimal.
- ✅ Tap + twice → upper stepper shows `2`, LotPicker pill shows **`2 / 4`** with the lane next to your date row reading **`2 / 4`** (the unit hint `1 = × 500 g` shows once in the picker header).
- ✅ Confirm → outbox flushes, stock drops to `2`.

**Failure signals** (any of these = bug):
- Total line reading "4 g".
- LotPicker showing "Taken 2 of 4 g" or "× 500 g" repeated three times.
- Decimal stepper allowed.

---

## Test 2 — Flip a SKU to divisible

Pick one of the 4 `pack_size=1, unit=kg` SKUs (cornflakes, turmeric, salt, or toor dal). These are safe candidates because `pack_size=1` means the unit interpretation doesn't change.

**Zero out stock first** (server blocks the flip otherwise):
- Field-app → EditStock → set qty to 0 → save. Wait for sync.

**Flip in the dashboard**:
- Catalog tab → expand the row → click **Edit**.
- The expansion shows **TRACKING MODE** with a checkbox `Divisible — allow decimal dispense in kg`. Check it.
- Click **Save**.
- ✅ Row shows a small `Divisible` badge next to the pack size.

**Verify the flip is blocked when stock exists**:
- Receive 1 pack on the field-app first.
- Try to uncheck Divisible in the dashboard → click Save.
- ✅ Should return a 409 error toast: *"Cannot change Divisible while stock or open lots exist…"*

---

## Test 3 — Divisible-mode receive & dispense

Continue with the SKU you just flipped (`pack_size=1, unit=kg`).

**Receive 3 packs (= 3 kg since pack_size=1)**:
- Field-app → Receiving → qty `3`, pick expiry → save.
- ✅ Stock screen shows `3 kg` (no `× 1` repetition since `dispense_mode=divisible`).

**Dispense 1.5 kg**:
- DispenseScreen → select SKU.
- ✅ Card reads **`3 kg on hand`**.
- ✅ Big stepper accepts decimals (tap the number → keypad → type `1.5`).
- ✅ Confirm → stock drops to `1.5 kg`.

**Dispense remaining 1.5 kg in two takes from different lots**:
- First receive another 2 kg with a *later* expiry so you have two lots (1.5 kg earliest, 2 kg later).
- DispenseScreen → set qty to 2.5.
- ✅ LotPicker auto-allocates `1.5` from earliest lot, `1` from later.
- ✅ Per-lot inputs accept decimals (tap and type `0.5`).
- ✅ Edit the per-lot inputs to `1.5 / 1.0`. Upper stepper syncs to `2.5`.
- Confirm.

---

## Test 4 — Real divisible product (`pack_size > 1`)

Create a new catalog SKU with `unit=kg, pack_size=5, dispense_mode=divisible` from the dashboard's "New product" form. (Or field-app registration: scan a fresh barcode, fill name + 5 kg pack size; the **TRACKING MODE** chip row should appear → pick `Divisible (decimal kg)`.)

**Receive 2 packs** (intake is always whole packs, even in divisible mode):
- ReceivingScreen → qty `2`, expiry → save.
- ✅ Stock shows **`10 kg`** (2 packs × 5 kg). This is the key test: pack-intake → base-unit storage.
- ✅ EditStock opens with a single batch of `10 kg` (decimal field, not stepper).

**Dispense 1.5 kg** (a partial of one pack):
- DispenseScreen → qty `1.5` → confirm.
- ✅ Stock drops to `8.5 kg`.

**Verify the "opened pack" representation is implicit**:
- Stock view shows just `8.5 kg`. That's "1 unopened 5kg pack + 1 pack opened with 3.5 kg remaining". The math reads off the number; no extra column needed.

---

## Test 5 — Cross-device sync (multi-device)

If you have a second phone or simulator:

1. Receive 5 kg on phone A.
2. Pull-to-refresh on phone B.
3. ✅ B shows `5 kg` with `dispense_mode=divisible` (the JIT/listStock response carried the field).
4. Dispense 1 kg on B.
5. Pull on A.
6. ✅ A shows `4 kg`.

---

## Test 6 — Dashboard chip rendering

Receive a multi-batch divisible product (3 + 4 + 3 packs with different expiries). On the dashboard's **Stock** tab:
- ✅ Expiry column shows three urgency-colored pills, format `2 × 29 Aug 2026 · in 4 months` (not the old `2@2026-08-29`).
- ✅ Pills tinted red if expired, amber if ≤30 days, neutral otherwise.

---

## Test 7 — Sheet export

Run the **Sync to Sheet** button on the Stock tab. Open the Google Sheet:
- ✅ The "Expiry dates" column shows `2 × 29 Aug 2026, 2 × 29 Nov 2026` — not the cryptic `@` format.

---

## Quick sanity script (run while testing)

After each action, this query tells you the truth from the DB:

```sql
SELECT
  s.barcode,
  s.name,
  s.on_hand,
  COALESCE(cp.dispense_mode, ip.dispense_mode, 'pack') AS mode,
  COALESCE(cp.pack_size, ip.pack_size) AS pack_size,
  s.unit,
  (SELECT json_agg(json_build_object('exp', l.expiry_date, 'qty', l.qty))
     FROM inventory_stock_lots l
    WHERE l.site_id = s.site_id AND l.barcode = s.barcode AND l.qty > 0) AS lots
FROM inventory_stock s
LEFT JOIN product_barcodes pb ON pb.site_id = s.site_id AND pb.barcode = s.barcode
LEFT JOIN canonical_products cp ON cp.product_id = pb.product_id
LEFT JOIN inventory_products ip ON ip.site_id = s.site_id AND ip.barcode = s.barcode
WHERE s.name ILIKE '%<your product name>%';
```

For pack mode: `on_hand` is integer pack count, lots `qty` is pack count.
For divisible: `on_hand` and lots `qty` are in base units (kg/ml/g).

---

**Bottom line for "did it work":**
- Pack-mode SKUs behave exactly like before (no decimal, displays show `N × packSize unit`).
- A flipped SKU lets the field-app accept decimals at dispense, stores qty in base units, and the dashboard/sheet show the new readable format.
- The flip is gated by stock — you can't accidentally corrupt a live SKU.

Run Test 1 first; if anything in pack mode broke, stop and report — that's a regression. Then proceed through 2 → 7.

```
1	/**
2	 * Global "Offline — showing saved data" banner.
3	 *
4	 * Mounted once above the navigator in `RootNavigator`. Shows whenever
5	 * `Network.getNetworkStateAsync().isConnected === false`, hides as soon
6	 * as connectivity returns. Stays out of the way otherwise (zero height
7	 * when online — no layout shift).
8	 *
9	 * We listen via `addNetworkStateListener` rather than polling so the
10	 * banner appears within ~1 s of the radio losing signal. As a safety
11	 * net we also poll once on mount because the OS event isn't guaranteed
12	 * to fire on cold start.
13	 */
14	
15	import { WifiOff } from 'lucide-react-native';
16	import * as Network from 'expo-network';
17	import React, { useEffect, useState } from 'react';
18	import { StyleSheet, View } from 'react-native';
19	import { SafeAreaView } from 'react-native-safe-area-context';
20	
21	import { Text } from '../design';
22	import { useT } from '../i18n';
23	import { useTheme } from '../theme';
24	
25	export function OfflineBanner() {
26	  const t = useT();
27	  const { palette } = useTheme();
28	  const [offline, setOffline] = useState<boolean>(false);
29	
30	  useEffect(() => {
31	    let unsubscribe: { remove: () => void } | null = null;
32	
33	    // One initial probe so we don't miss the "boot offline" case (the
34	    // event listener only fires on transitions).
35	    Network.getNetworkStateAsync()
36	      .then((s) => setOffline(!s.isConnected))
37	      .catch(() => {});
38	
39	    try {
40	      unsubscribe = Network.addNetworkStateListener((state) => {

46	    }

63	        </Text>

66	  );
67	}
```

> AGENT

Two bugs in the offline detection:

1. `expo-network`'s `isConnected` can be `undefined` on some Android devices and the listener never fires reliably — `setOffline(!undefined)` becomes `true`, so we get stuck "offline".
2. No "back online" transient signal — the banner just disappears.

Best fix: also treat successful API calls as ground truth (if a request just succeeded, we're online no matter what expo-network thinks). Plus the green transition.

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01QMWLyQvN78CvSocx4a6DZK
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/appointy/work/OfficeOperationsUmbrella/field-app/src/sync/networkState.ts",
  "content": "/**\n * Authoritative \"are we online?\" signal for the field-app.\n *\n * Why this exists:\n *   `expo-network` alone is unreliable on mid-range Android devices —\n *   `isConnected` sometimes returns `undefined` or stays `false` even\n *   when the radio is up and API calls are succeeding. The old\n *   OfflineBanner just `!isConnected`'d that into a permanently-stuck\n *   \"Offline\" banner.\n *\n * Strategy here:\n *   • Track the timestamp of the last successful API request.\n *   • If a request succeeded recently (within `RECENT_SUCCESS_MS`),\n *     consider the device online regardless of what expo-network says.\n *   • Otherwise fall back to expo-network. Treat `undefined` as online\n *     (assume best; false positives are worse than false negatives).\n *   • Re-probe periodically so we recover when the radio comes back\n *     between transitions the OS forgot to emit.\n *\n * The api client (`sync/api.ts`) calls `markApiSuccess()` after every\n * 2xx response and `markApiNetworkError()` on `Network request failed`.\n */\n\nimport * as Network from 'expo-network';\n\nconst RECENT_SUCCESS_MS = 20_000; // 20 s: if we got a 200 in that window, we're online.\nconst REPROBE_MS = 15_000; // poll expo-network every 15 s as a backup.\n\ntype Listener = (online: boolean) => void;\n\nlet lastApiSuccessAt = 0;\nlet lastApiErrorAt = 0;\nlet probedOnline: boolean | null = null; // null until first probe finishes.\nlet currentOnline = true; // optimistic default.\nconst listeners = new Set<Listener>();\n\nfunction recompute(): void {\n  const now = Date.now();\n  const recentSuccess = now - lastApiSuccessAt < RECENT_SUCCESS_MS;\n  // A recent success is the strongest signal — if the server replied in\n  // the last few seconds, the network is up. Beats expo-network because\n  // some devices misreport on the radio side.\n  const next = recentSuccess\n    ? true\n    // Else: use the probe if we have one, otherwise default to online.\n    // Critically we DO NOT flip offline when the probe is `null`.\n    : probedOnline !== false;\n  if (next !== currentOnline) {\n    currentOnline = next;\n    for (const fn of listeners) {\n      try { fn(currentOnline); } catch { /* swallow */ }\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nasync function probe(): Promise<void> {\n  try {\n    const s = await Network.getNetworkStateAsync();\n    // Treat undefined as \"not enough info\" → leave probedOnline at its\n    // previous value, OR if never set, leave it null so the override\n    // logic above defaults to online.\n    if (typeof s.isConnected === 'boolean') {\n      probedOnline = s.isConnected;\n    }\n  } catch {\n    // Probe failed — leave the previous value, don't flip the user offline\n    // just because expo-network errored.\n  } finally {\n    recompute();\n  }\n}\n\n/** Public hook: called by the API client after a successful response. */\nexport function markApiSuccess(): void {\n  lastApiSuccessAt = Date.now();\n  recompute();\n}\n\n/** Public hook: called by the API client after a network-layer failure\n *  (DNS, socket, fetch threw). NOT for 4xx / 5xx — those mean we got to\n *  the server. */\nexport function markApiNetworkError(): void {\n  lastApiErrorAt = Date.now();\n  // Probe immediately to refresh the OS signal — we don't trust a cached\n  // \"online\" if the radio just dropped.\n  void probe();\n}\n\nexport function isOnline(): boolean {\n  return currentOnline;\n}\n\nexport function subscribeNetworkState(fn: Listener): () => void {\n  listeners.add(fn);\n  // Fire once with the current state so the subscriber doesn't have to\n  // also call isOnline() to initialise.\n  try { fn(currentOnline); } catch { /* swallow */ }\n  return () => {\n    listeners.delete(fn);\n  };\n}\n\nlet started = false;\n\n/** Boot the background probe. Called once at app start. */\nexport function startNetworkProbe(): void {\n  if (started) return;\n  started = true;\n  void probe();\n  setInterval(() => { void probe(); }, REPROBE_MS);\n  try {\n    Network.addNetworkStateListener((state) => {\n      if (typeof state.isConnected === 'boolean') probedOnline = state.isConnected;\n      recompute();\n    });\n  } catch {\n    // Older expo-network builds: silently rely on the polling probe.\n  }\n}\n\n// For tests / debug.\nexport function _resetForTests(): void {\n  lastApiSuccessAt = 0;\n  lastApiErrorAt = 0;\n  probedOnline = null;\n  currentOnline = true;\n  listeners.clear();\n  started = false;\n}\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/appointy/work/OfficeOperationsUmbrella/field-app/src/components/OfflineBanner.tsx",
  "content": "/**\n * Top connectivity banner.\n *\n * Two visual states:\n *   • Offline  → red bar, \"Offline — showing saved data\". Stays until\n *                connectivity returns.\n *   • Back online (transient) → green bar, \"Back online\". Auto-hides\n *                after a few seconds. Only appears as the transition\n *                from offline → online, not on steady-state online.\n *\n * The signal comes from `sync/networkState`, which combines:\n *   1. Recent successful API calls (strongest signal — beats the radio).\n *   2. `expo-network`'s probe + listener as a fallback.\n *\n * That fixes the previous bug where mid-range Android devices returned\n * `isConnected === undefined` and got stuck on the red banner forever.\n */\n\nimport { WifiOff, Wifi } from 'lucide-react-native';\nimport React, { useEffect, useRef, useState } from 'react';\nimport { StyleSheet, View } from 'react-native';\nimport { SafeAreaView } from 'react-native-safe-area-context';\n\nimport { Text } from '../design';\nimport { useT } from '../i18n';\nimport { useTheme } from '../theme';\nimport {\n  isOnline as readIsOnline,\n  startNetworkProbe,\n  subscribeNetworkState,\n} from '../sync/networkState';\n\nconst BACK_ONLINE_VISIBLE_MS = 2500;\n\nexport function OfflineBanner() {\n  const t = useT();\n  const { palette } = useTheme();\n  const [online, setOnline] = useState<boolean>(readIsOnline());\n  const [showBackOnline, setShowBackOnline] = useState(false);\n  const prevOnline = useRef<boolean>(online);\n  const backOnlineTimer = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null>(null);\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    startNetworkProbe();\n    const unsub = subscribeNetworkState(setOnline);\n    return () => {\n      unsub();\n      if (backOnlineTimer.current) clearTimeout(backOnlineTimer.current);\n    };\n  }, []);\n\n  // Detect the offline → online transition and flash the green banner.\n  useEffect(() => {\n    const wasOffline = prevOnline.current === false;\n    if (wasOffline && online) {\n      setShowBackOnline(true);\n      if (backOnlineTimer.current) clearTimeout(backOnlineTimer.current);\n      backOnlineTimer.current = setTimeout(\n        () => setShowBackOnline(false),\n        BACK_ONLINE_VISIBLE_MS,\n      );\n    }\n    prevOnline.current = online;\n  }, [online]);\n\n  if (!online) {\n    return (\n      <SafeAreaView edges={['top']} style={{ backgroundColor: palette.error }}>\n        <View style={styles.bar}>\n          <WifiOff size={16} color={palette.onError} strokeWidth={2.4} />\n          <Text variant=\"labelLarge\" color={palette.onError} style={{ marginLeft: 8 }}>\n            {t('offlineBanner')}\n          </Text>\n        </View>\n      </SafeAreaView>\n    );\n  }\n\n  if (showBackOnline) {\n    // Reuse the warn palette as a soft \"good\" fallback if the theme\n    // doesn't define an explicit success color. Looks distinct from the\n    // red offline state without needing a new token. (Most themes here\n    // use warm yellows; if you later add a `success` token, swap this in.)\n    const successBg = (palette as { success?: string }).success ?? '#16A34A';\n    const successFg = '#FFFFFF';\n    return (\n      <SafeAreaView edges={['top']} style={{ backgroundColor: successBg }}>\n        <View style={styles.bar}>\n          <Wifi size={16} color={successFg} strokeWidth={2.4} />\n          <Text variant=\"labelLarge\" color={successFg} style={{ marginLeft: 8 }}>\n            Back online\n          </Text>\n        </View>\n      </SafeAreaView>\n    );\n  }\n\n  return null;\n}\n\nconst styles = StyleSheet.create({\n  bar: {\n    flexDirection: 'row',\n    alignItems: 'center',\n    justifyContent: 'center',\n    paddingVertical: 6,\n    paddingHorizontal: 12,\n  },\n});\n"
}
```

Clean. Summary of both fixes:

## 1. The Metro syntax error

The error was from a transient state during my last set of edits — Metro hot-reloaded a file mid-write. The file on disk parses clean now (TS compiler verified). Restart the dev server (`r` in Metro or `npx expo start -c` to nuke its cache) and the error will be gone.

## 2. The stuck-offline banner

**Root cause**: `expo-network`'s `isConnected` sometimes returns `undefined` on mid-range Android. The old code did `!s.isConnected` → `!undefined` → `true` → permanent "Offline" banner. The OS listener also doesn't always fire transitions, so reloading didn't help.

**Fix** (`src/sync/networkState.ts`):
- **Recent API success is the strongest signal.** If any request to your backend returned a response (even an error response — the radio is up) in the last 20 s, we're definitively online. This overrides whatever `expo-network` says.
- **`undefined` from `expo-network` is treated as online** (false positives are worse than false negatives — a banner that won't dismiss is more annoying than one that briefly misses an outage).
- **Periodic re-probe every 15 s** as a backup in case the OS listener silently drops.

**Wiring**: the API client (`request<T>` and the login fetch in `sync/api.ts`) calls `markApiSuccess()` after every response and `markApiNetworkError()` when fetch itself throws.

**New green "Back online" transient** in `OfflineBanner.tsx`:
- Red `WifiOff` banner while offline.
- On the **offline → online transition**, a green `Wifi` "Back online" banner shows for **2.5 s**, then auto-hides. Steady-state online renders nothing.
- Uses `palette.success` if your theme defines it, falls back to `#16A34A`.

Type-check clean. Not committed.
